## C001 — Abstract randomized-group populations equal analyzed populations

**Cited location found:** Yes. Abstract INTERVENTIONS and RESULTS (p. 1); Figure 1 allocation and primary-analysis boxes (p. 3); Statistical Analysis and Results population descriptions (p. 4). [jama_bluth_2019_oi_190055_16092.pdf — PDF p. 1](<../../../jama_bluth_2019_oi_190055_16092.pdf#page=1>); [jama_bluth_2019_oi_190055_16092.pdf — PDF p. 3](<../../../jama_bluth_2019_oi_190055_16092.pdf#page=3>); [jama_bluth_2019_oi_190055_16092.pdf — PDF p. 4](<../../../jama_bluth_2019_oi_190055_16092.pdf#page=4>)

**Source printed value/text matched:** Yes, except expressly narrowed reconstructed input claims identified below. Abstract: randomized high n=989 and low n=987; Figure 1: 1011 and 1002 randomized/allocated, 989 and 987 included in primary analysis; Results: 2013 randomized, 1976 analyzed.

**Comparator matched:** Explicit allocation counts 1011/1002 and total 2013 versus abstract randomized-labelled counts 989/987.

**Consistency rule applicable:** Exact population-label and allocation-count identity; randomized denominators and analyzed denominators must be distinguished when their printed values differ.

**Calculation or logical comparison reproduced:** 989 + 987 = 1976; 1011 + 1002 = 2013; high difference 22, low difference 15; 22 + 15 = 37 = 2013 − 1976.

**Necessary inputs available and exact missing inputs/definitions:** None for literal count/label comparison. Intended abstract referent remains unnamed.

**Source-grounded alternative interpretation:** The abstract can be read as a compressed description of analyzed treatment groups; its RESULTS explicitly distinguishes 2013 randomized from 1976 completing the trial. That does not make the INTERVENTIONS n values the allocation counts.

**Direct observation versus inferred explanation:** Direct: the population labels and four arm counts. Inferred: analyzed sizes may have been inserted into the allocation description; no production mechanism is established.

**Exact remaining human question:** Should the abstract INTERVENTIONS n values describe the allocated groups (1011/1002) or the analyzed groups (989/987)?

**Compared document roles:** REPORT main article versus REPORT main article. Each cited abstract, figure, table, narrative, completed-analysis method or footnote is REPORT content, by what it describes rather than its filename.

**Category:** Analysis-unit or population inconsistency

**Relationship type:** S_W

**All relationships:** S_W

**Review domain:** statistical_results

**Classification rationale:** All discrepancy locations are in the main trial REPORT, including its completed-analysis methods and results footnotes. Primary type and all relationships remain S_W. No PLAN or other REPORT document supplies a substantive conflicting comparator; corroboration does not add a relationship. Precedence P_R > P_P > S_C > S_W therefore leaves S_W. No document-role, primary-type, all-relationship or category change is required.

**Classification ambiguity:** None for document role or relationship type; bounded computation/population/display questions are recorded in the evidence card.

**Evidence state:** recorded_candidate

**Determining recheck fact:** The exact source retains randomized wording with analysis sizes and separately prints larger allocation sizes.

**Suggested review disposition:** retain (mechanical evidence-state derivation only).

**Human review status:** Pending Human Adjudication.

**Comparator matched:** The same arm's two printed denominators 986 and 987 and complementary-labelled category sum.

**Consistency rule applicable:** A single mutually exclusive/exhaustive abdominal-versus-nonabdominal classification has one observed denominator. Applicability is conditional on these rows being one classification, rather than independently missing binary indicators.

**Calculation or logical comparison reproduced:** 894 + 92 = 986; printed denominators differ by one (986 versus 987). The sum equals 986 and differs from 987 by one.

**Exact remaining human question:** For the H arm, are abdominal and nonabdominal entries categories of one mutually exclusive variable; if so, which common denominator applies, and if not, what explains the separate denominators?

This is risk of high-group membership conditional on event versus no event, a different contrast from event risk conditional on treatment.

**Necessary inputs available and exact missing inputs/definitions:** Counts, reference populations, label, CI, and generic named approximation available. Missing: exact point-estimate formula, event/reference coding, whether an adjusted/standardized estimand is intended, and display rule. It is a source-grounded numerical explanation, not evidence that the authors used that coding. A differently defined adjusted/standardized RR could also differ from the crude event ratio, but its target/formula must be supplied. The Wald-approximation wording alone does not define a different point contrast.

**Direct observation versus inferred explanation:** Direct: count/reference cells, risk-ratio heading, point/interval and generic calculation wording. ## Direct-source procedure and derivative record

Direct Poppler 26.01.0 pdftotext -layout extracted current physical PDF pages 4, 9 and 10; Poppler pdftoppm rendered current pages 9 and 10 at 160 dpi. Both current renders were visually read for headers, row alignment, numerators, denominators, percentage cells, RR points, CI endpoints and footnotes. Native/layout text was checked against those renders. CPU only; no OCR or fallback was needed. Sources/reused artifacts were not edited. The current main-source SHA-256 a76e0d8789cfcdbb51d86edaaf407e29ada6f2c0d3a2a8a7b95bb50565d12bc1 matches source_hashes_before.sha256.

Table 3 values are [Main article — PDF p. 9](../../../jama_bluth_2019_oi_190055_16092.pdf#page=9) and [Main article — PDF p. 10](../../../jama_bluth_2019_oi_190055_16092.pdf#page=10). Figure 2 is [Main article — PDF p. 10](../../../jama_bluth_2019_oi_190055_16092.pdf#page=10). Physical PDF numbers, not printed journal-page numbers, are used.

## Calculation boundaries

A crude binary-event RR is (High events/High patients)/(Low events/Low patients). A nearest two-decimal RR display x admits an underlying value approximately in [x−0.005,x+0.005); endpoint tie conventions do not change any result here. This is a conditional identity check, not substitution for an undisclosed fitted estimator. The paper also states complete-case outcome analysis, but Table 3 supplies reference denominators unless otherwise indicated. A row-specific complete-case denominator would need confirmation.

A diagnostic uses the same four source-linked cells with transposed conditioning: q = [High events/(High events+Low events)]/[(High patients−High events)/(High patients+Low patients−High events−Low events)]. It compares the proportion allocated High PEEP among events with that among nonevents. Several printed values resemble q, sometimes after truncation. This observation suggests an explicit orientation question; it does not establish software usage, cause, estimator intent or a correction. A q agreement does not turn q into the intervention event-risk RR named by the surrounding text. CI/P comparisons and their variance rules are not adjudicated by this diagnostic.

- **Consistency rule applicable:** Conditionally yes: No./total No. (%) gives the exact proportion identity; nearest one-decimal rounding is the registered display rule, but truncation is an alternative.
- **Calculation/logical comparison reproduced:** Yes, as the registered conditional calculation. 100 × 7/31 = 22.580645%; nearest one-decimal display 22.6%, whereas 22.5% is printed; difference −0.080645 percentage points. The nearest-rounding interval for 22.5% is [22.45%, 22.55%), which excludes the exact quotient. 
- **Necessary inputs available:** Yes for exact count-based arithmetic and the displayed comparator. No for choosing between nearest rounding, truncation, rounded-input subtraction where applicable, or an alternative computation.

- **Necessary inputs available:** Yes for the count-defined arithmetic and the transposed diagnostic: events, reference totals, group labels and RR point/interval are supplied. No for identifying the exact fitted/conditioned estimand.
- **Exact missing inputs or definitions:** Methods PDF p. 4 states that heterogeneity analyses add treatment × subgroup interaction terms to a generalized linear model with a binomial distribution. It does not specify this row's link function, adjustment set or mapping from that model to the displayed subgroup point and CI. The interaction P value is shared by the two category rows; it is not a test of this row's effect. No interaction-P/CI identity is asserted.
- **Source-grounded alternative interpretation:** The reported binomial interaction model allows a fitted-estimate interpretation; the exact subgroup link/contrast is not supplied. From the printed counts, the transposed diagnostic q = 0.967472. An orientation change, a separately fitted/standardized contrast, or another display convention could explain a different point. These remain alternatives, not source-stated production mechanisms.
- **Direct observation versus inferred explanation:** Direct: the specified row, group headers, numbers, label and cited method/footnotes. Derived: the crude event RR, its nearest-rounding comparison and the transposed diagnostic. A table-orientation or model explanation is inferred and unconfirmed; no adjustment, continuity rule or particular test is assumed.
- **Exact remaining human question:** For Waist-to-hip ratio <1.0, what exact event/reference orientation, population denominator, estimator and display rule produced RR 0.97 (0.83–1.13)? Is it the High/Low PEEP event-risk contrast from 97/467 and 100/457, a fitted contrast, or the differently conditioned quantity shown in the diagnostic?
- **Compared document roles:** REPORT main article Figure 2 Waist-to-hip ratio <1.0 event counts/denominators versus REPORT main article same row Risk Ratio point/interval; REPORT main article Methods PDF p. 4 provides analysis/label context.
- **Category:** Measure, label, or scale inconsistency.
- **Relationship type:** S_W.
- **All relationships:** S_W.
- **Review domain:** report_internal_inconsistency.
- **Classification rationale:** All compared values, labels and completed-analysis descriptions are REPORT content in the same main article. No PLAN location is a conflicting comparator and no second REPORT document is a conflicting comparator. S_W is the sole discrepancy relationship, so precedence selects S_W. The registered category and derived domain are confirmed; the roles are made location-specific.
- **Classification ambiguity:** None about content role or relationship; the estimator/display definitions listed above remain scientific input limitations.
- **Evidence state:** recorded_candidate.
- **Evidence-state determining fact:** The directly confirmed event counts and denominators yield 0.949229, outside the nearest-rounding interval for printed 0.97. The registered label/estimator/display question remains pending its named definition. No transcription is disproved and the conditional registered question is preserved.
- **Suggested review disposition:** retain, derived mechanically from recorded_candidate; Pending Human Adjudication.

## C082 — Conditional thoracic VAS moment bound

**Cited location found:** Yes. [eTable 7 low day 2 thoracic — PDF p. 28](<../../../joi190055supp4_prod_16092.pdf#page=28>); [10 cm VAS definition — PDF p. 17](<../../../joi190055supp1_prod_16092.pdf#page=17>); [Postoperative VAS assessment — PDF p. 20](<../../../joi190055supp1_prod_16092.pdf#page=20>).

**Source printed value/text matched:** Yes. Low day-2 VAS thoracic—cm, mean (SD), No.: 1.4 (3.9), 841; high1.3 (0.8),838, P=.34.

**Comparator printed value/text matched:** Yes. Protocol p.17 names Visual Analogue Scale (10 cm) for dyspnea/thoracic/abdominal pain; p.20 repeats postoperative assessment but no numeric endpoints.

**Consistency rule applicable:** Applicable only if postoperative scores are zero-origin 0–10 cm distances on the protocol line: sample variance ≤n/(n−1) m(10−m).

**Calculation or logical comparison reproduced:** At m1.4,n841, maximum SD=3.47194. Allowing mean rounding up to1.45 gives 3.52310, below minimum rounding-consistent SD3.85 by 0.32690 cm.

**Necessary inputs available:** Mean, SD label, n, cm and 10 cm line length are available.

**Exact missing inputs or definitions:** Numerical scoring origin/endpoints, postoperative identity with the baseline-defined scale, transformation and SD convention.

**Source-grounded alternative interpretation:** A shifted 10 cm interval does not yield the mean-dependent 0–10 bound; its universal maximum SD is about5.00. Different range/transformation could reconcile the tuple.

**Direct observation versus inferred explanation:** Direct: moment tuple and 10 cm plan wording. Conditional inference: zero-origin 0–10 postoperative support. No standard external VAS endpoints are assumed.

**Category:** Numeric or arithmetic inconsistency

## C088 — Mild respiratory failure sensitivity-forest point versus crude risks

**Cited location found:** Yes. [eFigure 12 Mild respiratory failure — PDF p. 42](<../../../joi190055supp4_prod_16092.pdf#page=42>); [Table 3 component counts — PDF p. 9](<../../../jama_bluth_2019_oi_190055_16092.pdf#page=9>); [Table 3 footnote b — PDF p. 10](<../../../jama_bluth_2019_oi_190055_16092.pdf#page=10>); [Individual component sensitivity plan — PDF p. 3](<../../../joi190055supp3_prod_16092.pdf#page=3>).

**Source printed value/text matched:** Yes. Forest Risk Ratio (99.58% CI): 0.92 [0.76,1.12].

**Comparator printed value/text matched:** Yes. Main high135/989 and low154/987; final SAP expressly specifies a generalized linear model for individual-component sensitivity analysis with 99.58% Bonferroni intervals.

**Consistency rule applicable:** Crude RR identity is applicable to main counts, but the equality check against this separately fitted sensitivity point needs an estimator/population alignment not supplied by the sources.

**Calculation or logical comparison reproduced:** (135/989)/(154/987)=0.874851; absolute distance from0.92=0.045149, larger than .005. Interval is positive, ordered and contains its printed point (0.76≤0.92≤1.12). This diagnostic does not reproduce a fitted GLM.

**Necessary inputs available:** Printed forest point/CI, main component counts/populations, RR labels and GLM sensitivity family are available.

**Exact missing inputs or definitions:** Sensitivity model link/formula, adjustment or standardization, event/contrast coding, realized analysis population/denominators and point-estimator output.

**Source-grounded alternative interpretation:** A model-based/standardized ratio under a distinct analysis population or estimator can differ from the raw main-arm ratio. Bonferroni width alone does not explain a point shift, but model definition can.

**Direct observation versus inferred explanation:** Direct: point/CI/counts and explicit sensitivity GLM plan. Derived: crude main-count diagnostic. Unsupported inference: sensitivity GLM point is the crude marginal RR calculated from main counts.

**Exact remaining human question:** For Mild respiratory failure, what realized GLM/link/contrast/adjustment/population produced0.92, and does its RR mean the crude main-arm ratio or a model-defined ratio?

**Category:** Measure, label, or scale inconsistency

**Compared document roles:** REPORT results supplement eFigure 12 component sensitivity estimate versus REPORT main Table 3 event counts/footnote; PLAN final SAP specifies sensitivity GLM and corroborates context. Roles confirmed by content: completed-trial results/methods are REPORT; prospective protocol, amendment and statistical plan are PLAN.

**Relationship type:** S_C

**All relationships:** S_C

**Review domain:** report_internal_inconsistency

**Classification rationale:** Completed-trial article and non-plan results supplement are REPORT content. The registered primary comparison crosses these REPORT documents; any PLAN context corroborates and creates no P_R.  No document-role or relationship-code change from registration; role descriptions above make the substantive locations explicit.

**Classification ambiguity:** Registered cross-REPORT relation retained; missing estimator/population alignment is explicit. PLAN specifies model context and corroborates, creating no P_R discrepancy.
